CHEATSHEET: predicting 50-gene expression (log(1+count)) from 224x224 px H&E patches (0.5 um/px, 112 um square) of human skin melanoma
====================================================================================================
Source: one Xenium section of cutaneous melanoma, aggregated to spots (one spot = one patch), 814 spots.
Values are natural log(1+count). All numbers below come from that training section.

CONTENTS
 1. Quick summary / strategy
 2. Section anatomy and the six tissue classes (visual description + how to recognise)
 3. Per-class expression profiles (mean, sd) for all 50 genes  <-- core lookup table
 4. Global per-gene statistics and quantiles
 5. Gene modules / correlations (use to keep predictions internally consistent)
 6. Effect of empty (white) background in the patch
 7. Expected accuracy of image-feature models per gene (how much to trust image signal)
 8. Python feature-extraction code (exact) + recommended pipeline
 9. REFERENCE TABLE: 814 training spots -> 22 image features + class + all 50 genes (compact encoding)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. QUICK SUMMARY / STRATEGY
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* The single biggest determinant of expression is WHAT TISSUE fills the patch:
  - melanoma tumour nests (most patches, ~53%): high POSTN, VIM, MGP, TYRP1, HAPLN1 (5-6.5), LYZ ~3; keratinocyte genes low (~1-1.7).
  - tumour with lymphocyte/stroma infiltration (~11%): like tumour plus immune genes up (CD52, LCK, GZMK, ITM2A, WDFY4, BIRC3, IDO1, CCL19 ~1.3-2.8, LYZ ~5.4) and HAPLN1 lower (~2.2).
  - epidermis (squamous epithelium; ~6% full-thickness + ~5% basal/junction): keratinocyte genes very high (KRT16/KRT17/DSP/KRT5 6-7.5, S100A14, SBSN, KRTDAP, SLPI, MAL2, AQP3 5-6), highest total counts of all.
  - cornified / superficial epidermis & keratin (~3%): differentiation genes (SBSN, KRT16/17, SLPI, KRTDAP, DMKN ~4.5-6.5) but basal genes (TP63, KRT5, COL17A1, LAMB3) lower, tumour genes low.
  - acellular / empty / dense collagen / keratin slab / blank glass (~22%): everything low (sum over 50 genes ~23 vs ~77 in tumour). Fully blank patches still have small non-zero values (mean sum ~15).
* Melanoma signature: TYRP1 (melanocytic), VIM, POSTN, MGP, HAPLN1, CRABP2 moderately; these are high in essentially every cellular non-epidermal patch in this section.
  POSTN/VIM/MGP are also stromal, so cellular stroma also scores high on them.
* Predict per class (Section 3) and blend by the estimated area fraction of each tissue type in the patch; then scale down roughly in proportion to the fraction of
  blank background (Section 6). If you can run Python, compute the features of Section 8 and use the reference table of Section 9 (random forest or kNN, k~5-10),
  which gave mean Pearson r ~0.54 across genes in spatially blocked cross-validation; combining that with your own visual classification of epidermis vs tumour should help,
  because simple features confuse epidermis with tumour (see Section 7).
* If in doubt about a patch, the tumour-class (T) profile is the safest default: it is the majority class.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. SECTION ANATOMY AND THE SIX TISSUE CLASSES
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The section is a nodular melanoma: a large dermal mass of sheets/nests of large epithelioid melanoma cells (big round/oval vesicular nuclei, prominent nucleoli,
abundant pale pink-purple/amphophilic cytoplasm, nests separated by thin fibrovascular septa with small vessels and occasional red blood cells), covered at the edges
by epidermis (stratified squamous epithelium with a thick eosinophilic compact cornified layer / keratin, plus some loose basket-weave keratin flakes).
There are a few lymphocyte-rich zones (small dark round nuclei among tumour cells / along strands of stroma) and some necrotic/discohesive areas.
Classes were defined by k-means clustering of expression (6 clusters) and then inspected visually:

 T   (tumour, n=433, 53%): solid nests/sheets of large epithelioid cells, round vesicular nuclei with nucleoli, pinkish-purple cytoplasm, little white space,
      sometimes thin septa or a small vessel. Also includes some discohesive/necrotic tumour patches and a few tumour-edge patches with clear space.
      Total (sum of 50 log values) 77 +- 9.
 TI  (tumour + immune/stroma, n=88, 11%): tumour nests interspersed with many small dark round lymphocyte nuclei and/or streaming spindle-cell stroma and
      fibrillary pink strands with slit-like spaces; or sheets of small dark round cells. Total 86 +- 13.
 LOW (acellular / empty, n=180, 22%): blank glass (white), patch edges, loose detached keratin flakes (wavy pink ribbons), dense homogeneous pink acellular collagen or
      compact keratin slab with very few, flat nuclei, sparse loose connective tissue. Total 23 +- 12 (range 0-61).
 EPI (epidermis, full, n=51, 6%): stratified squamous epithelium - polygonal keratinocytes with abundant glassy pink cytoplasm, evenly spaced smaller oval nuclei,
      intercellular bridges, granular layer (purple keratohyalin granules) under the pink compact cornified layer; includes basal layer with dense palisaded
      darker nuclei; occasional koilocyte-like clear cells / melanoma cells in the epidermis (pagetoid). Total 167 +- 17 (the highest).
 BAS (basal epidermis / epidermis-tumour junction, n=38, 5%): mix of epidermis (basal/spinous layers, dense darker nuclei) and underlying tumour/stroma,
      or adnexal epithelium; keratinocyte basal genes high but differentiation genes moderate; tumour genes still moderately high. Total 137 +- 18.
 KER (superficial epidermis / cornified, n=24, 3%): compact eosinophilic (bright pink) cornified layer, flattened upper keratinocytes with elongated nuclei
      arranged in parallel lamellae, often with white background in part of the patch. Total 103 +- 21.

Visual decision hints:
 - Bright homogeneous pink with almost no nuclei: either LOW (if far from living epidermis, e.g. thick keratin slab or collagen) or KER (if flattened
   keratinocyte nuclei in parallel layers, or the pink layer sits on top of living epidermis). Ambiguous -> average LOW and KER (keratin slabs with only a few
   nuclei were mostly LOW in this section; parallel lamellae of flattened nucleated cells were KER).
 - Large round nuclei with nucleoli, cells in nests, purple-pink cytoplasm, no intercellular bridges -> T.
 - Same plus many small (lymphocyte-size, ~6-8 um = 12-16 px) very dark round nuclei, or wavy stromal strands -> TI.
 - Keratinocytes (glassy pink cytoplasm, smaller regular nuclei, bridges, layered architecture, keratin on top) -> EPI; if mainly basal layer + dermis/tumour -> BAS.
 - Mostly white glass -> LOW with values scaled by tissue fraction.

Notes on within-class variation:
 - Within T, the main axis is HAPLN1 (with MGP): HAPLN1 is bimodal (~0.7-1.5 vs ~4-5.5). HAPLN1-high tumour patches are denser (less white space,
   more pink, more nuclei: mean white 0.05 vs 0.16, nuclei count higher). HAPLN1-low tumour tends to be looser, more open/oedematous, or near the upper
   (superficial) part of the mass. 74% of T patches have HAPLN1 > 2.5. Without other cues predict ~3.8 for T.
 - Second axis within T: a little keratinocyte signal (KRT5/KRT16/KRT17/DSP ~2-3) when the patch is near the epidermis.
 - Third axis: vascular/immune (VWF, ITM2A, LYZ, CD52) - patches with visible vessels (RBC-containing lumens, endothelium) have VWF ~2.5-4 and MMRN1 ~1-2.
 - VWF top patches: open vessels with red cells, loose oedematous stroma with capillaries.
 - LOW: the total scales with how much cellular tissue is in the patch; acellular collagen/keratin slabs ~15-25 total; blank glass ~0-20.

Principal components of expression: PC1 (47% var) = keratinocyte/epidermis (KRT16, DSP, KRT17, KRT5, S100A14, SERPINB5 load +).
PC2 (29%) = tumour/stroma (POSTN, VIM, MGP, TYRP1, HAPLN1, LYZ +). PC3 (4%) = immune (LYZ, CD52, ITM2A, WDFY4, BIRC3 +; HAPLN1 -).
So most variance is captured by two numbers: 'how much epidermis' and 'how much tumour/cellular dermis'. Keep predictions consistent with these modules:
e.g. if you predict KRT5 high, also predict KRT17/KRT16/DSP/S100A14/TP63/COL17A1 high.

-> Simple features nail LOW vs tissue and T, but mix EPI/BAS with T and KER with LOW, and under-call TI. Visual judgement of
   'is this squamous epidermis?' and 'are there many lymphocytes?' is where you can add the most value. Keratinocyte genes are worth
   getting right: they span 0 -> 7+ and the epidermis patches dominate their variance.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. FEATURE CODE (exact code used to make the reference table) AND RECOMMENDED PIPELINE
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Requires numpy, pillow, scikit-image, scipy (sklearn for the model). Patches: RGB 224x224, 0.5 um/px.

import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from skimage.color import rgb2hed
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
FEATS=['white','R','G','B','RmB','Hm','Em','Hp90','Ep90','nuc','nn','nsz','nbig','nsmall','dark','pink','lpink','tex','tex2','gsd','G_sd']
def feats(path):
    a=np.asarray(Image.open(path).convert('RGB')).astype(float)/255
    r,g,b=a[...,0],a[...,1],a[...,2]; gray=a.mean(-1)
    white=(gray>0.85)&(a.max(-1)-a.min(-1)<0.1); tis=~white
    t=tis if tis.sum()>50 else np.ones_like(tis)
    hed=rgb2hed(a); H=hed[...,0]; E=hed[...,1]
    f={}
    f['white']=white.mean()                      # fraction blank glass
    f['R']=r[t].mean(); f['G']=g[t].mean(); f['B']=b[t].mean()   # mean colour of tissue pixels
    f['RmB']=(r-b)[t].mean()                     # pink(+) vs purple(-)
    f['Hm']=H[t].mean()*100; f['Em']=E[t].mean()*100            # hematoxylin / eosin optical density
    f['Hp90']=np.percentile(H[t],90)*100; f['Ep90']=np.percentile(E[t],90)*100
    nuc=(gray<0.5)&(b>r*0.8); nuc=ndi.binary_opening(nuc)
    f['nuc']=nuc.mean()                          # nuclear area fraction
    lab,n=ndi.label(nuc); sz=np.bincount(lab.ravel())[1:]; sz=sz[sz>=15]
    f['nn']=len(sz); f['nsz']=np.median(sz) if len(sz) else 0   # nucleus count / median size (px)
    f['nbig']=(sz>120).sum(); f['nsmall']=((sz>=15)&(sz<60)).sum()
    f['dark']=(gray<0.35).mean()
    f['pink']=(tis&(r-g>0.25)&(gray>0.5)).mean()
    f['lpink']=(tis&(r-g>0.15)&(gray>0.7)).mean()
    f['tex']=np.abs(np.diff(gray,axis=0)).mean()+np.abs(np.diff(gray,axis=1)).mean()
    sm=ndi.uniform_filter(gray,9); f['tex2']=np.abs(gray-sm).mean()
    f['gsd']=gray[t].std()
    f['G_sd']=g[t].std()
    return f

(The table below has 21 features in this order: white R G B RmB Hm Em Hp90 Ep90 nuc nn nsz nbig nsmall dark pink lpink tex tex2 gsd G_sd.)

Parsing the reference table (Section 9): every data line starts with 'REF '. Fields separated by single spaces:
  REF <row_id> <class> <21 feature values> <expr>
where <expr> is a 100-character digit string: 50 two-digit integers, gene order as GENES below, value = int(two digits)/10 = log(1+count) rounded to 0.1.

GENES=['ACER1','AHNAK2','AQP3','BIRC3','CAPNS2','CCL19','CCL22','CD1B','CD52','COL17A1','CRABP2','DEFB1','DMKN','DSC1','DSP','FGFBP1','GZMK','HAPLN1','HBD','IDO1','IL1B','ITM2A','KRT16','KRT17','KRT2','KRT5','KRTDAP','LAMB3','LCK','LY6D','LYZ','MAL2','MGP','MIR205HG','MMRN1','MYH11','POSTN','RHOV','S100A14','SBSN','SELE','SERPINB2','SERPINB5','SLPI','TFF3','TP63','TYRP1','VIM','VWF','WDFY4']
import pandas as pd
rows=[l.split() for l in open('cheatsheet.txt',encoding='utf-8') if l.startswith('REF ')]
X=np.array([[float(v) for v in r[3:24]] for r in rows]); cls=[r[2] for r in rows]
Y=np.array([[int(r[24][2*i:2*i+2])/10 for i in range(50)] for r in rows])
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestRegressor
rf=RandomForestRegressor(500,min_samples_leaf=5,max_features=0.5,random_state=0).fit(X,Y)
# predict: rf.predict(np.array([[feats(p)[k] for k in FEATS] for p in paths]))

Recommended: (a) RF prediction from features; (b) your own visual class call (Section 2) -> class-mean profile (Section 3);
blend e.g. 0.5/0.5, or trust the class profile more when you are confident a patch is epidermis (EPI/BAS/KER) since features
under-detect epidermis. kNN alternative: standardise features (z-score using the table's mean/sd), k=5-10 neighbours, distance-weighted mean
(CV mean r 0.50). Be aware of possible stain/colour differences in new images; the most robust features are white, nuc, nn, tex, tex2, pink.
If colour looks shifted overall, rely more on structure and on visual classes.
